# Freedom of Information Act Frequently Requested Records/Proactive Disclosures | U.S. Department of Labor

This page contains proactive disclosures of frequently requested documents pursuant the Freedom of Information Act, 5 U.S.C. § 552(a)(2) and policy guidance from the U.S. Department of Justice, Office of Information Policy, *Proactive Disclosures of Non-Exempt Agency Information: Making Information Available Without the Need to File a FOIA Request* (USDOJ/OIP Mar. 16, 2015).

  ## Jordan v. U.S. Dep't of Labor, No. 16-1868 (D.D.C.)
  + [Redacted email string produced during FOIA litigation in *Jordan v. U.S. Dep't of Labor*](/sites/dolgov/files/OALJ/PUBLIC/FOIA/Frequently_Requested_Records/Jordan_v_DOL/DynCorp_Email_Chain_Powers_Huber_Email_Redacted.pdf), No. 16-1868 (D.D.C.) (July 30-31, 2013)
  > This record is being produced to the public under the Freedom of Information Act, 5 U.S.C. § 552 et seq. (“FOIA”). Portions of this record have been redacted as exempt under Exemption 4 of the FOIA. The redacted portions have been held to be attorney-client privileged under Exemption 4 by the D.C. Circuit. *Jordan v. U.S. Dep’t of Labor*,273 F. Supp. 3d 214, 232 (D.D.C. 2017), *reconsideration denied*, 308 F. Supp. 3d 24 (D.D.C. 2018), *motion for relief from judgment denied*, 2019 U.S. Dist. LEXIS 109455; 2019 WL 2744556 (D.D.C. July 1, 2019), *aff’d Jordan v. U.S. Dep’t of Labor*, No. 18-5128, 2018 WL 5819393 (D.C. Cir. Oct. 19, 2018). Publication of this record to the DOL’s public-facing website fulfills DOL’s obligation to provide this record to the public under 5 U.S.C. § 552(a)(2)(D) and (a)(3)(A).
  >
  > Additional individual requests for this record will not be processed, as “FOIA does not require agencies to produce duplicate records.” *Competitive Enter. Inst. v. Office of Sci. & Tech. Policy*, 241 F. Supp. 3d 14, 22–23 (D.D.C. 2017). Moreover, “[t]he statute is not a discovery tool that requires agencies to produce every conceivable copy in the possession of every governmental custodian.” *Jett v. FBI*, 139 F.Supp.3d 352, 365 (D.D.C. 2015). In addition, “[I]t would be illogical and wasteful to require an agency to produce multiple copies of the exact same document.” *Defs. of Wildlife v. Dep’t of Interior*, 314 F.Supp.2d 1, 10 (D.D.C. 2004). Further, “[w]here the records have already been furnished, it is abusive and a dissipation of agency and court resources to make and process a second claim.” *Dorsett v. U.S. Dep’t of Treasury*, 307 F. Supp. 2d 28, 33 (D.D.C. 2004) (citing *Crooker v. State Dep’t*, 628 F.2d 9, 11 (D.C. Cir. 1980) (*per curiam*)). “Once such documents are open for inspection by the general public, there is no longer any matter in controversy…under FOIA.” *Freedberg v. Dep’t of the Navy*, 581 F. Supp. 3, 4 (D.D.C. 1982).
